\end{array}\\[22.76228pt]
\mathcal{A}\Biggl(\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 24.98148pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\begin{matrix}\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Delta;y::\mathsf{M};r::\mathsf{N}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}::\mathsf{E}\\
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Delta\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}H}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}::\mathsf{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{G}\end{matrix}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\Delta\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}H}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}::\prescript{\sigma[\mathsf{M}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\mathsf{N}\rightarrow\mathsf{F}\}\!\!\}\Rightarrow\mathsf{E}$}}\par}\par}}}$},\\[14.22636pt]
\Gamma\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}H}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}:\prescript{\sigma[\mathcal{M}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{i}\}\!\!\}_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\mathcal{E}\end{array}\Biggl)&=&\hskip-2.84544pt\begin{array}[]{c}\exists\mathsf{G}\sqsubset\mathcal{G}_{i}.\,\exists\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma.\\
\forall i\in I.\,\exists\Gamma_{i}\subseteq\Gamma.\,(\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i})\\
\wedge\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{1},\Gamma^{\prime};y:\mathcal{M};r:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E})\\
\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{2},\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}H}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}:\mathcal{F}_{i}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{i})\\
\end{array}\\[28.45274pt]
\end{array}

Figure 12.
Description of the function
\mathcal{A}
.

When the underlying term is, for example, a
\lambda
-abstraction, the formula
\mathcal{A}(\Pi,J)
is naturally obtained as the logical conjunction of some formulas produced by applying the same algorithm to the subderivations of
\Pi
. When, instead, the term is an application, a fixed point, or a handled term, it is necessary to existentially quantify over the refinements of the type that such typing rule “hides” and that is not visible in the conclusion of
\Pi
. It is worth noting that the formula
\mathcal{A}(\Pi,J)
has been constructed so as to be interpretable solely on the basis of the objects
\Pi
and
J
and, therefore, is a closed formula. It thus makes sense to write
\models\mathcal{A}(\Pi,J_{r})
, to mean that such a formula is true.

The first thing we prove about the function
\mathcal{A}
is that the produced logical condition indeed captures the informal specification around which
\mathcal{A}
was constructed. This is expressed by the following result.

Proposition 5.5 (Correctness of
\mathcal{A}
).

Let
\Pi
be an
\mathsf{HEB}
type derivation with conclusion
J_{s}
and
J_{r}
an
\mathsf{HEBI}
type judgement such that
J_{s}\sqsubset J_{r}
. It holds that
\models\mathcal{A}(\Pi,J_{r})
if and only if there exists an
\mathsf{HEBI}
type derivation
\Xi
, with conclusion
J_{r}
, such that
\Pi\sqsubset\Xi
.

The function
\mathcal{A}
, in addition to returning a formula whose truth value is the expected one, is computable, which can be established by observing that all the quantifiers appearing in the produced formula range over sets which, thanks to Lemma
5.4
, we know to be finite. Consequently:

Proposition 5.6 (Computability of
\mathcal{A}
).

The function
\mathcal{A}
is computable, i.e., there exists an algoritm that given in input a type derivation
\Pi
and
a judgement
J
determine whether the formula
\mathcal{A}(\Pi,J)
holds.

We finally reached our target result.

Theorem 5.7 (Decidability).

The reachability problem for terms typable in
\mathsf{HEB}
is decidable.

6.
Back to
\HEPCF

In the previous section, we have seen how
\mathsf{HEBI}
can be regarded not only as a type system complete for termination and reachability, but also that such completeness properties are preserved, although in a relative sense, when
\mathsf{HEBI}
is viewed as a refinement language for
\mathsf{HEB}
. In this section, we will show how the very same technique can also be applied to
\HEPCF
.

In
Figure

13
, we define a refinement relation between
\HEPCF
and
\mathsf{HEBI}
types, as we already did for
\mathsf{HEB}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\\[-5.69046pt]
\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 16.47223pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\mathsf{n}\sqsubset\left\{\,\right\}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\par}\par}}}$}&&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hskip 11.94666pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$0<n\leq m\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\mathsf{m}\sqsubset\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\par&&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 7.9222pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\sigma:\mathsf{M}\mathrel{\rightsquigarrow}\mathsf{N}\in\mathtt{E}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\mathsf{M}\sqsubset\mathcal{M}$}}\hbox{$\left(\mathsf{N}\sqsubset\mathcal{N}_{i}\right)_{i\in I}$}}\hbox{$\left(\prescript{}{\mathtt{E}}{\mathsf{L}}\sqsubset\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)_{i\in I}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\prescript{}{\mathtt{E}}{\mathsf{L}}\sqsubset\prescript{\sigma[\mathcal{M}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\}\!\!\}_{i\in I}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}\\[14.22636pt]
\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 9.23413pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\left(\mathsf{M}\sqsubset\mathcal{M}_{i}\right)_{i\in I}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\left(\mathsf{U}\sqsubset\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)_{i\in I}$}}}}\hbox{$\mathsf{M}\rightarrow\mathsf{U}\sqsubset\left\{\mathcal{M}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}$}}\par}\par}}}$}&&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 43.83214pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\mathsf{M}\sqsubset\mathcal{M}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\prescript{}{\mathtt{E}}{\mathsf{M}}\sqsubset\prescript{\mathsf{return}[\mathcal{M}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\,\}\!\!\}$}}\par}\par}}}$}&&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.68059pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\hskip 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\mathsf{S}\sqsubset\mathcal{E}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\mathsf{U}\sqsubset\mathcal{F}$}}}}\hbox{$\mathsf{S}\Rightarrow\mathsf{U}\sqsubset\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}\\[14.22636pt]
\end{array}

Figure 13.
\HEPCF
Type Refinement

Following exactly the same recipe we used for
\mathsf{HEB}
, the refinement relation can be lifted from types to judgements and derivations in a very natural way. Moreover, reduction itself can be given on derivations rather than terms, following the recipe we used in
Section

5.3
for
\mathsf{HEB}
. All this leads quite quickly to the following:

Lemma 6.1 (Relative Subject Expansion for
\HEPCF
).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t},{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\texttt{Cmp}}}
be two closed terms such that

{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\rightarrow{}{}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}
. If there exists

\Xi^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}:\mathcal{E}
and
\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}::\mathsf{U}
such that
\Pi^{\prime}\sqsubset\Xi^{\prime}
and also there exist
\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}::\mathsf{U}
such that
\Pi\rightsquigarrow\Pi^{\prime}
,
then there exists

\Xi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}
such that
\Pi\sqsubset\Xi
.

We can therefore conclude that, for the purposes of the reachability problem, the search space of
\mathsf{HEBI}
derivations for a given
\HEPCF
program typed through
\Pi
can be restricted to those
\mathsf{HEBI}
derivations that refine
\Pi
. Why does this
not
contradict the undecidability of the reachability problem for
\HEPCF
? Simply because the finite refinement property cannot hold for
\HEPCF
, as can be easily verified by inspecting the rules in
Figure

13
. Indeed, let us consider the
\HEPCF
computation type
\prescript{}{\mathtt{E}}{\mathsf{1}}
where the effect context
\mathtt{E}
attributes
\sigma
the type
\sigma:\mathsf{1}\mathrel{\rightsquigarrow}\mathsf{1}
.
It is immediate to check that all the following
\mathsf{HEBI}
types refine
\prescript{}{\mathtt{E}}{\mathsf{1}}
:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{E}_{0}\coloneq\prescript{\mathsf{return}[\left\{\mathsf{1}\right\}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\,\}\!\!\}&\mathcal{E}_{n+1}\coloneq\prescript{\sigma[\left\{\mathsf{1}\right\}]\!}{}{\{}\!\!\{\left\{\mathsf{1}\right\}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{n}\}\!\!\}\end{array}

7.
Related Work

7.1.
Intersection Type Systems

Since their introduction
(
11
)
fifty years ago, intersection types have not only been a type-theoretic foundation of ad-hoc polymorphism
(
45
)
, but also a powerful tool for analyzing the semantic properties of higher-order programs, when the latter are expressed as
\lambda
-terms or related calculi.
In languages as diverse as Java
(
8
)
, Scala
(
37
)
, and Typescript
(
6
)
, variations on the theme of intersection types have been adopted in order to provide type systems with a degree of flexibility that is incompatible with classical parametric polymorphism.

The original idea of intersection types (namely, to allow a single term to be assigned a type capturing different and completely unrelated types) has been generalized in various ways and over calculi that extend the
\lambda
-calculus along heterogeneous directions.
It is worth recalling, in this context, the so-called non-idempotent intersection types
(
14
)
, which make it possible to capture quantitative refinements of the usual termination property, such as the number of steps that possibly different abstract machines would take to evaluate the term being typed
(
14
;
1
)
.
Concerning the axis of programming languages, it is also worth mentioning the extension of intersection types to languages with control operators, such as the
\lambda\mu
-calculus
(
58
)
, and, more recently, their extension to a calculus with algebraic effects
(
19
)
.
The latter, however, as already observed, is fundamentally different from the system introduced in this paper, in that effects are pre-interpreted and given meaning through the notion of monad
(
35
)
, whereas in the present work the role traditionally played by monads is instead taken by handlers. This means that there is no need to capture, say, probabilities, non-determinism, or exceptions in a native way, and the main difficulty becomes the interaction between operations and handlers.

The use of intersection types is seen as a decision algorithm in so-called higher-order model checking (HOMC) was pioneered by Kobayashi
(
31
)
, and has subsequently been studied in various directions, from its connection with the relational model of linear logic
(
23
)
to its link with linearity
(
10
)
. To the authors’ knowledge, the systems described in this article are the first examples of applying intersection types to HOMC within the setting of calculi with handlers.

7.2.
Reasoning about Programs Handling Effects

Algebraic effect handlers allow the user to implement effects within programs
in a structured, modular, and composable manner.
Their expressivity is underpinned by the ability to reify delimited
continuations as first-class values.
On the other hand, from the denotational perspective, algebraic effect handlers
can also be viewed as transformers of computation trees (or, equivalently,

free models
) over algebraic operations that underlying terms
perform
(
41
;
42
;
43
)
.
This view enables embedding algebraic effects and handlers in a
language
(
25
;
46
)
.

A major approach to reasoning about effectful higher-order programs is

(type-and-)effect systems
, and effect systems for algebraic effects and
handlers have been studied
extensively
(
41
;
42
;
4
;
24
;
34
;
59
;
56
)
.
However, these systems only keep track of what algebraic operations may
perform; in other words, they only approximate the algebraic operations
appearing in the computation tree produced by a program.
By contrast, our intersection type system
\mathsf{HEBI}
can precisely represent the
computation tree of a terminating program (non-terminating programs are ill
typed there).
This empowers us not only to characterize the termination of programs but also
to allow fine-grained typing.
For instance, as shown in
Section

2
,
\mathsf{HEBI}

allows different calls to the same algebraic operation to take arguments of
different types.
This typing is disallowed in the aforementioned effect systems, because those
systems have to typecheck all the calls to the same algebraic operation in a
uniform way, thereby requiring the arguments to have the same type.
Effect systems aware of the order of performed effects are called

sequential

(
52
;
51
;
21
;
22
)

or

temporal

(
33
;
36
;
48
;
50
)
,
depending on whether they reason about only the finite behavior or even the
infinite behavior of effectful programs.
These effect systems are interested in what
unhandled
—namely,
built-in—effects are performed in what order, whereas
\mathsf{HEBI}
can
track the order—and even branching—of
handled
effects as well as how it affects the
transformation of computations along with effect handlers.

Dijkstra
monads

(
53
)
are one of
the approaches that utilize the free model view, enabling the verification of
higher-order effectful programs by monadically transforming specifications to be
verified against the programs.
Their aim is at verification rather than typability, so the typing is not
as fine-grained as our type system and no work on Dijkstra monads considers
typing properties specific to intersection type systems, such as subject
expansion.

Higher-order model checking is also extended to support algebraic effects and
handlers by
13
with the undecidability result,
followed by the attempts to identify fragments where the HOMC problem is
decidable
(
49
;
47
;
17
)
.
These results on the decidable fragments rely on the CPS transformations that
translate a program with algebraic effect handlers to one without them.
Our work is also aligned with this direction, providing a more semantic viewpoint
than the justification with the CPS transformations.
This provides a benefit that
\mathsf{HEB}
accommodates terms that are not in the
fragments identified by the prior work, although they are incomparable:
only
\mathsf{HEB}
typecheckes computation
\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{1};x.\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{2};y.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}))
with values
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{2}
of different types, whereas
only
\mathsf{HEB}
cannot typecheck
\mathsf{case}(x;\sigma_{1}(x_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{1}.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1}),\sigma_{2}(x_{2};{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}_{2}.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{2}))
for
\sigma_{1}\neq\sigma_{2}
.

7.3.
Effect handlers and Session Types

Several works in the literature have already investigated the strong connections between effect handlers and session types (e.g.,
(
39
;
57
)
). In our setting, this relationship is reflected in the resemblance between session types and the tree of possible effects associated with computations in our systems.
However, there are important differences. In particular, session types place a central emphasis on notions of
choices
and their duality, which fundamentally structure the interaction. In
\mathsf{HEBI}
, this emphasis is not present: the distinction between branches is not primarily captured through dual choice constructs, but rather arises through intersections.

8.
Conclusion

This work introduces
\mathsf{HEBI}
, the first example of an intersection type system specifically designed for a calculus with handlers and algebraic effects. Noticeably,
\mathsf{HEBI}
is not merely an intersection type system, but it is also
behavioral
, in the sense that from the type of any computation one can deduce not only which algebraic operation will be executed next, but also what the type(s) of the underlying continuation must be. On the one hand, this makes it possible to retain all relevant information inside the types. On the other hand, this guarantees
compositionality
: computations and the handlers responsible for managing them can be typed independently, aggregating the corresponding type information only when needed.

What is remarkable about
\mathsf{HEBI}
is not only its precise characterization of terminating computations, but also the fact that it naturally suggests a new monomorphic type system (meaning that the same handler can not use the continuation in type-theoretically incompatible ways), namely
\mathsf{HEB}
, for which the reachability problem is decidable, contrary to what happens in similar type systems.

\HEPCF
for example, derives its expressive power primarily from
not
tracking the types of algebraic operations’ continuations within the computation type, thereby allowing the latter to become arbitrarily complex. This phenomenon is ultimately responsible for the undecidability of the HOMC problem in
\HEPCF
, as shown in
Section

6
. The use of behavioral forms of computation types in
\mathsf{HEB}
prevents this phenomenon to happen. At the same time,
\mathsf{HEB}
supports a form of polymorphism in the parameter and return types of algebraic operations that is not available in
\HEPCF
.
The decidability result on
\mathsf{HEB}
is established by making essential use of
\mathsf{HEBI}
itself, employing it as a refinement of
\mathsf{HEB}
, in the style of the results by Kobayashi and coauthors concerning the use of intersection types in the context of the HOMC problem.

There are certainly several promising directions for future research that the introduction of
\mathsf{HEBI}
naturally suggests.
First of all, it is worth mentioning that the previously discussed fragments of
\HEPCF
for which the reachability problem is decidable were shown to be so by defining suitable CPS translations and proving that these translations are type preserving. Studying such type systems through the refinement induced by
\mathsf{HEBI}
would undoubtedly lead to a deeper understanding of their expressive power, something which at present is not possible. Another important direction concerns the expressive power of the calculus
\mathsf{HEB}
, which in this article has been introduced as a kind of proof of concept, but is certainly of independent interest, especially if the behavioral types it comprises were to become recursive, similarly to what happens with session types.
Finally, similarly to what happens with intersection types and the
\lambda
-calculus,
\mathsf{HEBI}
types seem to induce a denotational model for
\mathsf{HEB}
that is worth studying in its own right. In particular, it would be worthwhile to study its relationship with the standard denotational semantics of effect handlers, which is based on trees in the free monad. They are in fact in close relationship, since an
\mathsf{HEB}
computation type can be inhabited only by a denotational tree in which all branches perform the same effects in the same order and finally return values of the same type. Note that the argument values of the effects performed in and the values returned by the branches may be different from each other: the branches only share which effect is performed in which order, and that, after the aforementioned sequence of effects, some value will be returned.

Acknowledgements.

We would like to express our gratitude to Victor Arrial and Mano Orenga for their valuable contributions during the initial phase of this work.

Appendix A
Proofs of Section
4

In this appendix, we provide detailed proofs of all the statements presented in the main text. The organization of this appendix closely follows that of the main article, with sections arranged accordingly.

We begin by defining the notion of
clash
in the
\mathsf{HE}
calculus. Clashes correspond to terms that are not in normal form, yet no further computation step can be performed. Most of these situations arise from typing errors.
The clashes are defined with the following form (
Fig.

14
).

\begin{array}[]{r l}\underline{n}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\\[2.84544pt]
\mathsf{case}(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u};{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{m})\\[2.84544pt]
\mathsf{case}(\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v};{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{m})\\[2.84544pt]
\mathsf{case}(\underline{n};{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{m})&\text{ where }n\leq 0\text{ or }n>m\\[2.84544pt]
\mathsf{with}\,{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\,\mathsf{handle}\,\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v};x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t})&\text{ where }\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}\}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\\[2.84544pt]
\mathsf{with}\,\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}\}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\,\mathsf{handle}\,\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v};x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t})&\text{ where }\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}\}\in{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\\
&\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}s}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}\end{array}

Figure 14.
The different kinds of clashes in
\mathsf{HE}
terms

Both the definitions of clashes for
\mathsf{with}\,{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\,\mathsf{handle}\,\sigma({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v};x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t})
extends easily in the case of
\mathsf{return}
clauses.

A.1.
Section
4.3

Remark A.1 (Type Context Simplification).

Let
p\in\Lambda
be term and
x
a variable that is not free in
p
.
If
\Gamma\vdash p:\mathcal{E}
and
x:\mathcal{M}\in\Gamma
, then
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\,\right\}
. In particular, if
p
is closed, then
\Gamma
is empty.

Lemma A.2 (
\mathsf{HEBI}
, Typability of Values).

For
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\texttt{Val}}}
, there exists

\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\left\{\,\right\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
.
∎

Corollary A.3.

For any value
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\texttt{Val}}}
, the type derivation

\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\left\{\,\right\}

defined in the previous lemma (
Lemma

A.2
)
is the smallest possible derivation for
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
.
In other words, for any
\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}
,

\Pi^{\prime}
has at least the size of
\Pi
.

See
4.1

Proof.

By case analysis on
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\texttt{Val}}}
, the
form of
\Pi
can be deduced :

Case Integers

For
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\underline{n}
,
\Pi
has the following form :

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\text{$\mathtt{(int)}$ }$}}}\hbox{\hskip 9.25003pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\mathcal{M}\in\{\left\{\,\right\},\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}\}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\Xi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\underline{n}:\mathcal{M}$}}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

Then necessarily
\mathcal{M}_{i}\in\{\left\{\,\right\},\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}\}
for each
i\in I
,
thus we can choose
\Xi_{i}
as the following :

\mathtt{(int)}

\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\underline{n}:\mathcal{M}_{i}

And conclude
\Gamma_{i}=\emptyset
for each
i\in I
.

Case Variable

For
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=x
,
\Pi
and
\Xi
are as following :

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.68744pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\text{$\mathtt{(var)}$ }$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}x:\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}x:\mathcal{M}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

By hypothesis
\mathcal{M}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
for each
i\in I
.
Thus we can choose each
\Xi_{i}
as following :

\mathtt{(var)}

x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}x:\mathcal{M}_{i}

And conclude that
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}=+_{i\in I}(x:\mathcal{M}_{i})=\Gamma=x:\mathcal{M}
.

Case Abstraction

For
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.52164pt\hbox{}}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\lower 3.21385pt\vbox{\vskip 3.01385pt\hrule height=0.4pt\vskip 3.01385pt\par}}\enskip\hbox{\lower 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\text{$\mathtt{(abs)}$ }$}}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\left(\Pi^{k}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{k}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t};x:\mathcal{N}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}_{k}\right)_{k\in K}\hfil\lx@intercol$}}\hbox{$\Xi\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{k\in K}\Gamma^{k}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}$}}\par}\par}}}$}\end{array}

We have
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}
so for each
i\in I
,
\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
with
J_{i}\subseteq K
and
K=\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}J_{i}
.
Let
\Gamma_{i}=+_{j\in J_{i}}\Gamma^{j}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}
,
we can choose
\Pi_{i}
as the following for each
i\in I
:

\mathtt{(abs)}

\left(\Xi^{j}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{j}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t};x:\mathcal{M}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}_{j}\right)_{j\in J_{i}}
\Xi_{i}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{j\in J_{i}}\Gamma^{j}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}

Therefore
\Gamma=+_{k\in K}\Gamma^{k}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}=+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}=+_{i\in I}+_{j\in J_{i}}\Gamma^{j}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}
.

Case Fixpoint

For
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
,
\Pi
has two possible shapes:

Base case :

\mathtt{(fixb)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma;x:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}
\Gamma\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}

Recursive case : For
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
:

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}

In both cases we have
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{k}\right\}_{k\in K}
so for each
i\in I
,
\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
with
J_{i}\subseteq K
and
K=\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}J_{i}
. By induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{1}
we obtain derivations
\Pi^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w};x:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow E_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
such that
\Gamma_{1}=+_{i\in I}\Gamma^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
. We can then build a derivation
\Pi_{i}
for each
i\in I
as following, for the recursive case:

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Xi^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w};x:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
\Gamma^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}+\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}

With
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}+\Gamma_{2}
.
In the base case the construction is similar. Therefore,
\Gamma=+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}
.

∎

Proof.

Base case :

\mathtt{(fixb)}

\Pi_{i}^{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};x:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}

Recursive case :
For
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{i}^{1}+\Gamma_{i}^{2}
:

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi_{i}^{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i}^{1};x:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}
\Pi_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{N}_{i}
\Gamma_{i}^{1}+\Gamma_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}

We first study the case in which all the
\Pi_{i}
are in base form.
By hypothesis,
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}
exists, therefore
+_{i\in I}(\Gamma_{i};x:\left\{\,\right\})
exists. Moreover,
\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}
also exists.
Then by induction hypothesis
on the
\Pi_{i}^{1}
, there is
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i};x:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}
.
One can conclude this subcase with the following
\Pi
.

\mathtt{(fixb)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i};x:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}

Otherwise, let’s assume there is at least one
\Pi_{i}
in recursive form. For each such derivation
\Pi_{i}
it exists a premise
\Pi^{2}_{i}
and its size is strictly less then the size of
\Pi_{i}
.
For every
\Pi_{i}
in base form, we can take as
\Pi^{2}_{i}
the derivation
\Pi_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\,\right\}
, that exists by typability of values (
Lemma

A.2
).
The proof size of
\Pi_{i}^{2}
is at most the size of
\Pi_{i}
(
Corollary

A.3
).
Since at least one
\Pi_{i}
is initially in recursive form, then the sum size of all the

\Pi_{i}^{2}
is strictly smaller than the sum size of all the
\Pi_{i}
.
Moreover, since each
\mathcal{N}_{i}
types the term
\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
, either
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\left\{\,\right\}
or
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J}
, therefore
\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{i}
always exists.
Thus by induction hypothesis on the
\Pi_{i}^{2}
, there exists

\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{i}
.
We now focus on the
\Pi_{i}^{1}
. As before, by hypothesis
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}^{1}
exists, therefore
+_{i\in I}(\Gamma_{i}^{1};x:\mathcal{N}_{i})
exists. Also,
\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}
exists.
Threfore, by induction hypothesis on the
\Pi_{i}^{1}
,
there is
\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}^{1},x:\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}

Therefore one can deduce the following
\Pi
:

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}^{1},x:\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}^{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{i}
+_{i\in I}(\Gamma_{i}^{1}+\Gamma_{i}^{2})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J_{i},i\in I}

∎

Since
\cup
is a partial relation, the following lemma ensures that it is always well defined for the types arising from derivations typing closed values.

Lemma A.4 (Union of Closed Values).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\texttt{Val}}}
a closed value,
\Pi
and
\Xi
two derivations such that
\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}
and
\Xi\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{N}
then
\mathcal{M}\cup\mathcal{N}
exists.

Proof.

By cases on
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
. If
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\underline{n}
then
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\left\{\left\{\,\right\},\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}\right\}
, thus
\mathcal{M}\cup\mathcal{N}=\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}
is defined.
Otherwise, being closed, we have either
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}=\mathsf{fix}\,x.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
. In both cases the types must have the form
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
and
\mathcal{N}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{j}\right\}_{j\in J}
. Therefore
\mathcal{M}\cup\mathcal{N}=\left\{\mathcal{M}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I\cup J}
.
∎

Finally, we must prove that the
\#
relation is well behaved with respect to substitution.

Remark A.5 (Compatibility of Substitution).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}\texttt{Hdl}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\in\Lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\texttt{Val}}}
and
x
a variable. Moreover, let
\psi
be either
\{\mathsf{return}(x)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\}
or
\{\sigma(y;r)\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\}
, then
\psi\mathrel{\#}{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}
if and only if
\psi\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}\mathrel{\#}{\color[rgb]{0.6992,0,1}h}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}
.

See
4.3

Proof.

By induction on
\Pi_{p}
. By case analysis on
p
, the
form of the
\Pi_{p}
can be deduced :

Case Integers

Let
p=\underline{n}
, so
\underline{n}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}=\underline{n}
and
\Pi_{p}
has the form:

\mathtt{(int)}

\mathcal{N}\in\{\left\{\,\right\},\left\{\mathsf{n}\right\}\}
\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\underline{n}:\mathcal{N}

So necessarily,
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\,\right\}
and
\Gamma_{p}=\emptyset
. Therefore, we take
\Pi=\Pi_{p}

Case Variable

•

If
p=x
,
x\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}={\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
and
\Pi_{p}
has the following form:

\mathtt{(var)}

\Pi_{p}\mathrel{\triangleright}x:\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}x:\mathcal{M}

Being
\Gamma_{p}=\emptyset
and
\tau=\mathcal{M}
. We then take
\Pi=\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
.

•

If
p=y\neq x
,
y\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}=y
and
\Pi_{p}
has the following form :

\mathtt{(var)}

\Pi_{p}\mathrel{\triangleright}y:\mathcal{N}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}y:\mathcal{N}

Therefore,
\Gamma_{p}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{M}=\left\{\,\right\}
. Finally, we take
\Pi=\Pi_{p}

Case Abstraction

For
p=\lambda y.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}
,
\Pi_{p}
has the following form :

\mathtt{(abs)}

(\Pi_{i}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{M}_{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}_{i})_{i\in I}
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i};x:\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\lambda y.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

By
Lemma

4.1
on
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
we can obtain some derivations
\Pi^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{i}
for
i\in I
.
By induction hypothesis on each
\Pi_{i}
with these
\Pi^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
, there exist derivations

\Pi^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{E}_{i}
.
Thus, one can derive the following
\Pi
.

\mathtt{(abs)}

(\Pi^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{E}_{i})_{i\in I}
+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\lambda y.{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

Case Fixpoint

For
p=\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
,
\Pi_{p}
has two possible shapes:

Base case :

\mathtt{(fixb)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma;x:\mathcal{M};y:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
\Gamma;\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

By induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{1}
with
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
, there exists a derivationr
\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma;y:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\right\}_{i\in I}
.
We can the construct the following derivation
\Pi
:

\mathtt{(fixb)}

\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma;y:\left\{\,\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\right\}_{i\in I}
\Gamma;\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

Recursive case :

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}_{1};y::\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
\Pi\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

By
Lemma

4.1
we can split the derivation
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
in two derivations
\Pi^{1}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\Pi^{2}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{2}
.
By induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{1}
with
\Pi^{1}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
, there exists some derivations
\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1};y:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\right\}_{i\in I}
.
Similarly, by induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{2}
with
\Pi^{2}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
, there exists some derivations
\Pi^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
.
We can therefore construct the following derivation
\Pi
:

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1};y:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\right\}_{i\in I}
\Pi^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

Case Let-Binding

For
p=\mathsf{let}\,y\doteq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}

has the following form :

\mathtt{(let)}

\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma;x:\mathcal{M}_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}

on

and

.

\Pi

\mathtt{(let)}

\Gamma_{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{let}\,y\doteq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{F}

Case Case

For
p=\mathsf{case}({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w};{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{n})

\Pi_{p}
has the following form :

\mathtt{(case)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathsf{m}\right\}

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2};x:\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{case}({\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w};{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}_{n}):\mathcal{E}

By
Lemma

4.1
we can split the derivation
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
in two derivations
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\sqsubset\Pi^{1}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\sqsubset\Pi^{2}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{1}
.
By induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{1}
with
\Pi^{1}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
and
\Pi_{2}
with
\Pi^{2}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
, there exist two derivations
\Pi^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathsf{m}\right\}
and

.

\mathtt{(case)}

By
Lemma

4.1
we can split the derivation
\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
in derivations

and
\Pi^{i}_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{i}
for each
i\in I
,.

\Pi_{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}
with

.
Moreover, by induction hypothesis on each
\Pi_{i}
with

for

, there exist derivations

.

\Pi^{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{1};y:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

\Gamma^{1}+\Gamma^{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

By induction hypothesis on
\Pi^{1}
, there exist a type
\mathcal{M}_{1}

and derivations
\Pi^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{1}

and
\Pi^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{1};y:\mathcal{N}^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
.
Similarly, by induction hypothesis on
\Pi^{2}
, there exist a type
\mathcal{M}_{2}

and derivations
\emptyset\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{2}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{2}

and
\Pi^{2}_{\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
.

Being
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}
closed, by
Lemma

A.4

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}
exists.
By intersection lemma (
Lemma

4.2
) on
\Pi^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}}
and
\Pi^{2}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}}
,
one can deduce
\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\emptyset\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}:\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}
.
Moreover, the following derivation
\Pi_{\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}}
holds.

\mathtt{(fixr)}

\Pi^{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{1};x:\mathcal{M}_{1};y:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
\Pi^{2}_{\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma^{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
\Gamma^{1}+\Gamma^{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{fix}\,y.{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}:\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

Case Let-Binding

For
p=\mathsf{let}\,y\doteq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}
,
\Pi
has the following form :

\mathtt{(let)}

\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Delta\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{E}
\varphi

.

{\mathcal{E}\mathrel{-\>\!\!\!\!\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{i}\right\}\>\!\!\!\!\overrightarrow{\,\vskip-8.69995pt{}^{i\in I}\;}}\mathcal{F}}

(\Pi_{i}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{G}_{i})_{i\in I}
\Delta+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}\mathsf{let}\,y\doteq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\mathcal{F}

By induction hypothesis on
\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}
, there exist types
\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}
and derivations

and
\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Delta;x:\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}
.
Similarly, by induction hypothesis on each
\Pi_{i}
, there exist types

\mathcal{M}_{i}
and derivations

and

.
Thus, one can derive the following
\Pi_{\mathsf{let}\,y\doteq{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}}
.

\mathtt{(let)}

\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Delta;x:\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}:\mathcal{E}
\varphi

.

{\mathcal{E}\mathrel{-\>\!\!\!\!\left\{\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{i}\right\}\>\!\!\!\!\overrightarrow{\,\vskip-8.69995pt{}^{i\in I}\;}}\mathcal{F}}

(\Pi_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{M}_{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}u}:\mathcal{G}_{i})_{i\in I}

Being
{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}

A.4

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}t}}\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}_{i}

4.2
) on the

Case Application

For
p={\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{2}
,
\Pi
has the following form :

\mathtt{(app)}

\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{1}\{x\backslash{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}v}\}:\left\{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\right\}

\Pi_{1}

and derivations

\Pi_{2}

Therfore, one can derive the following
\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{2}}

\mathtt{(app)}

\Pi_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{2}}\mathrel{\triangleright}\Gamma_{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}V}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{2}:\mathcal{N}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2};x:\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.44441pt\hskip-4.44441pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}\vdash}$}\hskip-4.44441pt\hskip-1.86803pt\raisebox{7.44444pt}{\hbox{$\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.4805,0.7773,0.0977}C}}}$}}\hskip-1.86803pt\hskip 4.44441pt}}{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{1}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0.9688,0.4766,0}w}_{2}:\mathcal{E}

) on
